# Biological Foundation Models Wiki

Last evidence review: 2026-10-10 · 486 entries across 10 categories.

A researcher-facing index of foundation models and explicitly labelled platforms or benchmarks for biological sequences, molecules, structures, omics, cells, and tissue images. Presence is not a recommendation, proof of reuse rights, or evidence of clinical fitness.

Audit state: **247 verified** and **239 verified with limits**. Every displayed row links to a dated source-review note in the canonical JSON. Unresolved candidates are excluded from this index and preserved in the repository's separate under-review register.

Legend: ⭐ = canonical / widely used in its area; NC = non-commercial terms apply to at least one artifact or access route. Always inspect the exact code, weight, data, and service terms.

| Borzoi ⭐ | 2024 | Verified | Calico convolutional model that predicts base-resolution RNA-seq coverage (plus ChIP/DNase/ATAC/CAGE) from ~524 kb of DNA as a unifying model of gene regulation; built on the Enformer architecture. | ~524 kb DNA window -> RNA-seq coverage at 32 bp resolution across many assays/tissues; derived variant scores | Open code + weights | [Nature](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41588-024-02053-6) · [GitHub](https://github.com/calico/borzoi) | eQTL/sQTL/paQTL/ipaQTL variant scoring, transcription/splicing/polyadenylation modeling, regulatory variant interpretation |
| Caduceus ⭐ | 2024 | Verified | Bidirectional long-range DNA language model with reverse-complement equivariance, based on Mamba-style blocks. | DNA sequence -> embeddings/log-likelihood/task outputs | Open code + weights | [ICML](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v235/schiff24a.html) · [GitHub](https://github.com/kuleshov-group/caduceus) · [project page](https://caduceus-dna.github.io/) | Variant effect, long-range regulatory modeling, RC-aware genomics |
| CpGPT ⭐ | 2024 | Verified | Transformer model pretrained on 150,000+ DNA methylation datasets for zero-shot methylation imputation, array conversion, aging biomarker prediction, and cross-tissue and cross-species transfer. | DNA methylation array profiles -> imputed methylation values, age predictions, cross-platform conversions | Open code + weights | [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2024.10.24.619766v3) · [GitHub](https://github.com/lucascamillomd/CpGPT) | methylation imputation, array platform conversion, epigenetic aging clocks, cross-tissue transfer, cross-species analysis |

Hugging Face Papers shows the first author's authorship of arXiv 2502.10807 claimed and verified by the Mishamq account. The GO-CRE preprint (bioRxiv 2026, co-authored by HybriDNA authors including Microsoft Research co-authors) names the GO-CRE repository as the HybriDNA implementation, and that repository calls the Mishamq checkpoints the published checkpoints. Hub configs match the layer and hidden-size table in arXiv v2; hub parameter counts are about 297.5M, 3.12B and 6.25B, so the paper's 7B variant holds about 6.3B parameters. Limits: a personal first-author release, not a Microsoft organisation release (the project page's microsoft/HybriDNA link is 404), and institutional authorisation is not documented. That the uploads are the exact paper checkpoints rests on the authors' statements. The 3B card text is copied from the 300M card, though its config is correct. Apache-2.0 for the weights appears in hub metadata, cards and the first-author README (badge and License section); none of these repositories has a LICENSE file. The GO-CRE copy of the model code is MIT. Inference needs Hugging Face remote code plus mamba-ssm and flash-attn. GitHub LICENSE.md (CC BY-NC-SA 4.0) covers the JAX code; HF weights and the PyTorch code repo (InstaDeepAI/ntv3_base_model) are auto-gated, licence 'other'; MODEL-LICENSE.md returns 401, so terms come from the card's licence summary (non-commercial only; output and derivative-model restrictions). NTv3 benchmark dataset auto-gated, CC BY-NC 4.0. Pre/post cards carry a paper placeholder; the generative card links the bioRxiv paper. Separate from bfm-nucleotide-transformer (v1/v2). | InstaDeep multi-species genomic foundation model with a U-Net-like architecture and single-base tokenization, pretrained on 9 trillion bp from OpenGenome2 with contexts up to 1 Mb and post-trained on about 16,000 functional tracks and annotations from 24 animal and plant species. Released as 8M, 100M and 650M pre-trained and 100M and 650M post-trained checkpoints; a fine-tuned generative (masked-diffusion) variant supports enhancer design. | DNA sequence (up to 1 Mb, length a multiple of 128), plus a species token for post-trained models -> masked-token predictions, embeddings, functional-track (BigWig) and genome-annotation (BED) predictions | JAX code on GitHub under CC BY-NC-SA 4.0; PyTorch model code and weights on Hugging Face behind an automatic gate under a custom InstaDeep non-commercial model licence (full text readable only after accepting the gate) | [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2025.12.22.695963v1) · [GitHub](https://github.com/instadeepai/nucleotide-transformer) · [Hugging Face (NTv3 650M post-trained)](https://huggingface.co/InstaDeepAI/NTv3_650M_post) · [Hugging Face (NTv3 650M pre-trained)](https://huggingface.co/InstaDeepAI/NTv3_650M_pre) · [Hugging Face (NTv3 generative)](https://huggingface.co/InstaDeepAI/NTv3_generative) · [Hugging Face Space](https://huggingface.co/spaces/InstaDeepAI/ntv3) | Cross-species functional-track prediction, genome annotation, fine-tuning for regulatory and annotation tasks, variant-effect interpretation, controllable enhancer design |
| PlantCaduceus (PlantCAD / PlantCAD2) | 2024 | Verified with limits: Re-verified 2026-10-10: Cell Genomics and PNAS DOIs via Crossref and Europe PMC (bioRxiv links the PlantCAD2 preprint to the journal DOI); GitHub Apache-2.0; Hugging Face cards, files and gating for 7 base checkpoints, 21 LoRA adapters and datasets. Limits: cell.com and pnas.org pages return 403 to automated fetch; the weight licence rests on card metadata only. The NC licences of the paper texts (bioRxiv CC BY-NC, PNAS CC BY-NC-ND) do not cover model artifacts. | Plant genomic foundation-model family; PlantCAD2 expands the successor model to 65 angiosperm species and 8,192-base context for transferable plant-regulatory representations. | Plant DNA window (up to 512 bp PlantCAD; up to 8,192 bp PlantCAD2) -> embeddings/masked-token predictions; zero-shot variant scores, fine-tuned annotations | Apache-2.0 code; public ungated PlantCAD (512 bp) and PlantCAD2 (8,192 bp) base checkpoints declare Apache-2.0 only in Hugging Face card metadata (no licence file); LoRA fine-tuned adapters and the Phytozome-derived 65-genome window dataset state no licence | [bioRxiv (PlantCAD2 v3)](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2025.08.27.672609v3.full) · [Cell Genomics (PlantCAD2)](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.xgen.2026.101329) · [PNAS (PlantCaduceus)](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.2421738122) · [GitHub](https://github.com/plantcad/plantcad) · [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/collections/kuleshov-group/plantcad2-67e437e241a382671371a572) | Cross-species splice/TIS/TTS annotation, deleterious-variant identification, transfer learning across diverged plant species (e.g. Arabidopsis->maize) |
| RAGenome | 2026 | Verified with limits: Discovery 2026-10-10; adversarial re-check 2026-10-10. arXiv v1 posted 2026-10-08 (ML4Molecules workshop, NeurIPS 2026); abstract links the GitHub repo, whose README points to the pantoniadis/RAGenome checkpoint (account of co-author Panagiotis Antoniadis). GitHub LICENSE MIT; HF card MIT, ungated, model.safetensors listed (~672 MB, consistent with 168M fp32). Required alignment dataset songlab/multiz100way-pigz is ungated and MIT-tagged. Trained with Homo sapiens as query species; the paper says other species would likely need further pretraining. Evaluated on two tasks only (BEND gene finding MCC 0.60; ClinVar AUROC 0.87, below GPN-MSA/GPN-Star). Earlier dnafm/168M_rag_* checkpoints are not referenced by the official repo and are not the release. | Retrieval-based genomic language model (168M parameters) that retrieves homologous sequences from a 100-vertebrate whole-genome alignment at pretraining and inference time, combining intra-species local attention with inter-species cross-attention to extend alignment-based gLMs to 13,312-nucleotide contexts. | Human (hg38) DNA query (up to 13,312 nt) plus retrieved aligned sequences from other vertebrates -> nucleotide likelihoods and embeddings; variant scores as reference/alternate log-likelihood ratios | Open code + weights (MIT), ungated; inference needs the 100-way whole-genome alignment (public MIT-tagged Hugging Face dataset) and the taxonomy metadata shipped in the repository; human (hg38) query only | [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11761) · [GitHub](https://github.com/PanosAntoniadis/RAGenome) · [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/pantoniadis/RAGenome) | Gene finding from frozen embeddings (BEND), zero-shot pathogenic-variant prioritization (ClinVar), evolution-aware representations of human genomic sequence |
| RegFM | 2026 | Verified with limits: Discovery and adversarial review both on 2026-10-10. MIT code and weights verified on GitHub and the Hub; ungated. The Hub has three undocumented checkpoint folders (AllProtein, HVG, PBMC), distinct by hash, all expression-prediction-stage ('genepred') models. AllProtein likely corresponds to the paper's full ENCODE all-protein-coding model, and PBMC to the leave-one-out demo (inferred from names). MLM-stage module checkpoints are not released. Released code covers gene-expression training and prediction only. Users must build tokenized DNA and TF/CR input features themselves. The preprint text is CC BY-NC-ND; that does not apply to the model artifacts. No checkpoint was downloaded or loaded. The card says the DOI will be added later. | Context-aware foundation model for human transcriptional regulation. It couples a 650 kbp gene-centred cis-DNA transformer (LongNet dilated attention) with a trans-regulator module that encodes the expression of 2,103 transcription factors and chromatin regulators, joined by cross-attention. Pretraining runs in stages: masked language modelling on the GRCh38 genome and on TF/CR expression, then expression prediction on ENCODE and CELLxGENE transcriptomes. The aim is to predict gene expression in unseen cellular contexts. | Gene-centred DNA sequence (650 kbp around the TSS) plus a 2,103-dimensional TF/CR expression profile for a cell context -> gene expression prediction and regulatory representations | Open code + weights (MIT), ungated; bundled customised transformers files keep their Apache-2.0 headers. Training data are third-party public (ENCODE, CELLxGENE) and not redistributed; no input-preprocessing code released | [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.744355v1) · [GitHub](https://github.com/ZjGaothu/RegFM) · [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Deku21/RegFM) | Gene expression prediction in unseen cell contexts (released scripts and demos); per the paper, fine-tuning for cis-regulatory element annotation, bivalent-promoter and dosage-sensitivity classification, and perturbation-response prediction, plus in silico TF knockout (pipelines not released) |
| SegmentNT · NC | 2024 | Verified with limits: set NC true and cite CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 model terms. | InstaDeep segmentation model built on the Nucleotide Transformer encoder + 1D U-Net that predicts the location of 14 classes of genomic elements at single-nucleotide resolution over up to 30 kb (generalizes to 50 kb). | DNA sequence up to 30 kb -> per-nucleotide segmentation across 14 element classes (genes, UTRs, exons/introns, splice sites, promoters, enhancers, CTCF, polyA) | Model hub; non-commercial terms apply to at least one artifact or access route | [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2024.03.14.584712v2) · [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/InstaDeepAI/segment_nt) | Single-nucleotide genome annotation, regulatory/gene-structure element localization, downstream regulatory genomics |
| TomatoPGFM | 2026 | Verified with limits: Adversarial review 2026-10-10. GitHub, Hugging Face, ModelScope and Zenodo are all listed in the preprint; the same model.safetensors (1,916,843,008 bytes, matching SHA-256) is on HF, ModelScope and Zenodo. Apache-2.0 is declared in README, MODEL_WEIGHTS.md, CITATION.cff and hub/Zenodo metadata; the shipped LICENSE omits several standard Apache-2.0 clauses (GitHub: NOASSERTION). Single-genus model; downstream tests are gene- and CDS-vs-intergenic classification on two held-out accessions (frozen probes and LoRA) with graph input disabled; PlantDNAMamba scored higher and DNABERT-2 lower in all four comparisons. Inference needs CUDA mamba-ssm; not Transformers-native. Nothing downloaded or executed. | Graph-conditioned genomic foundation model for tomato (479M parameters; bidirectional Mamba2 and local-attention mixture-of-experts blocks with a token-aligned graph adapter), pretrained by masked language modelling and graph-feature reconstruction on 54.65 Gb from 66 tomato accessions using pangenome node features and adjacency. | Tomato DNA sequence (reverse-complement-folded 6-mer tokens), optionally with token-aligned pangenome-graph node features and adjacency -> masked-token predictions and sequence embeddings; LoRA-adapted task predictions | Open code + ungated inference weights (Hugging Face, ModelScope, Zenodo); Apache-2.0 declared for both, but the shipped LICENSE is an abridged Apache-2.0 text; processed evaluation data CC BY 4.0; training-resume checkpoint not released | [bioRxiv](https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.08.31.748176) · [GitHub](https://github.com/tomatoai2026/TomatoPGFM) · [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/tomatoai2026/TomatoPGFM) · [Zenodo (weights v0.1.0)](https://zenodo.org/records/22032734) | Tomato sequence embeddings and LoRA fine-tuning (reported: gene- and CDS-versus-intergenic classification in two held-out accessions); graph-conditioned masked-token modelling of pangenome sequence |

| BiRNA-BERT | 2025 | Verified | 117M-parameter ncRNA transformer encoder trained on ~36M ncRNA sequences with adaptive dual tokenization (nucleotide-level NUC plus byte-pair encoding BPE) selected by input length, using ALiBi for context extension. | RNA sequence (NUC or BPE tokens, length-adaptive) -> embeddings and task predictions | Open code + weights (HF collection buetnlpbio/birna-bert) | [Communications Biology](https://www.nature.com/articles/s42003-025-08982-0) · [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2024.07.02.601703) · [GitHub](https://github.com/buetnlpbio/BiRNA-BERT) | Short-sequence RNA classification, long-context RNA modeling, fine-grained nucleotide-level structural prediction |
| CodonBERT | 2024 | Verified | BERT-style mRNA language model that tokenizes coding sequences by codons, pretrained on >10M mRNA sequences from diverse organisms for mRNA design and optimization (Sanofi). | mRNA coding sequence (codon tokens) -> contextual codon embeddings (and downstream regression/prediction heads) | Open code + weights (dual license: separate code and model-artifact licenses; PyTorch weights via download) | [Genome Research](https://genome.cshlp.org/content/34/7/1027) · [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2023.09.09.556981) · [GitHub](https://github.com/Sanofi-Public/CodonBERT) | mRNA vaccine/therapeutic codon optimization, protein expression prediction, mRNA stability/degradation prediction |
| ERNIE-RNA | 2024 | Verified | Structure-enhanced RNA language model incorporating base-pairing restrictions. | RNA sequence/structure-aware tokens -> embeddings, attention, structure outputs | Open code + weights | [Nature Communications](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-025-64972-0) · [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2024.03.17.585376v1.full-text) · [GitHub](https://github.com/Bruce-ywj/ERNIE-RNA) · [Hugging Face mirror](https://huggingface.co/multimolecule/ernierna) | Secondary structure, RNA functional annotation |

| La-Ribo | 2026 | Verified with limits: Independently re-verified 2026-10-10, two days after the preprint and both repositories appeared, so the release is very new and unreplicated (GitHub: one commit, 4 stars, no releases; HF: 13 downloads). MIT is declared in the GitHub LICENSE ('Copyright (c) 2025 La-Ribo contributors') and in the HF card metadata and footer; HF gated=false. Weight files were listed by the HF API, never downloaded or executed. Released code performs unconditional sampling only; the README states it does not run inverse folding, refolding or the designability evaluation. The released data are the 10,631 trRosettaRNA2-predicted structures (bpRNA-1m90 and RNAStrAlign sequences) with per-residue pLDDT, not the 168,561-structure training corpus, which is not released and which the authors say consists mostly of predicted or synthetic structures; they also report that only 0.31% of the corpus exceeds 160 nt and that strict success declines toward the top of the 40-150 nt benchmark. Designability, codesignability and inverse-folding figures are the authors' own, against their chosen baselines (gRNAde, NA-MPNN, RiboFlow-Mix) and two refolding models. Not checked: upstream licence terms of the folding models (trRosettaRNA2, OpenFold3, NuFold) whose predictions make up the corpus, and any peer review of the preprint. | Generative flow-matching model for joint RNA sequence and three-dimensional structure design: it samples a sparse phosphate-sugar-base scaffold together with residue-wise latents, and a shared scaffold-conditioned autoencoder decodes nucleotide identities and all heavy atoms. Two released flow checkpoints (with and without triangle pair updates) share that autoencoder prior, which the paper also uses for scaffold-conditioned inverse folding without additional training. | Target RNA length -> generated RNA sequence with all-heavy-atom 3D coordinates (PDB); scaffold conditioning is a paper protocol over the same weights, not a released script | Open code + weights (MIT), ungated; two flow checkpoints, shared autoencoder and normalisation statistics, plus a 10,631-structure predicted-structure dataset. Limits: there is no mRNA-FM-specific paper. The developers cite the 2022 RNA-FM preprint, whose full text does not mention mRNA-FM or the 45M-CDS corpus, so corpus and size figures rest on developer documentation. Year 2024 = first public loader commit in the official repository. | Codon-tokenized extension of RNA-FM released by the RNA-FM developers: a 12-layer BERT-style encoder (1,280 hidden dimensions, about 239M parameters) pretrained on about 45 million mRNA coding sequences to produce mRNA embeddings. | Codon-aligned mRNA coding sequence -> codon-level embeddings; downstream heads (e.g. expression prediction) | Open code + weights: MIT code (GitHub); mRNA-FM_pretrained.pth sits in the developers' ungated Hugging Face repository, whose card declares Apache-2.0 (the repository has no separate licence file). | mRNA-GPT (Kitasato) | 2025 | Verified with limits: Independently re-verified 2026-10-10. Ownership chain: the preprint's availability statement names only the code repository; that repository's owner is the paper's co-first author (GitHub account name ZHANG YIMING) and its README links the six Hugging Face checkpoints under the account ZYMScott (profile name 'yiming'), which is the author endorsement relied on here - the preprint itself does not mention Hugging Face. Checkpoints were published 2026-10-02, after the December 2025 preprint, and differ from the preprint's Methods in three details: context 2048 codons in config.json versus a 1,024-token window in the paper, rotary versus learnable positional encoding, and a 68-token vocabulary with BOS/EOS versus the paper's 69 items with CLS/SEP/MASK. Protein-constrained decoding is implemented and documented in the released code and model cards, while the preprint describes codon-constrained generation as future work. Three further ungated Apache-2.0 fine-tunes exist in the same namespace (bacterial-expression, fungal-expression, stability). The repository BibTeX entry has doi = {TODO}; the preprint DOI resolves and bioRxiv records no journal publication. A different, unrelated model is also called mRNA-GPT (Sanofi, bioRxiv 10.64898/2026.03.31.715707, posted 2026-04-02), so the name is qualified and the bare alias is deliberately omitted. Files were listed by API, not downloaded or loaded; no inference was run, so reported behaviour is documentation, not reproduction. | Family of decoder-only codon language models (302,109,696 parameters, 24 layers, 1024-dim, 16 heads, 68-token vocabulary of 64 codons plus special tokens), pretrained separately on bacterial, eukaryotic and archaeal coding sequences from NCBI RefSeq (about 80M, 83M and 2M CDSs). Three pretrained domain checkpoints plus three property fine-tunes, all downloadable without an account; no API route and no released training corpus (the corpus is rebuilt from NCBI RefSeq with the repository scripts). The record is reframed from the DRfold/DRfold2 structure-predictor entry to RCLM, the pretrained language-model component. This meets the recorded reconsider_when branch 'reframing around an evidenced reusable pretrained component'. The official standalone repository leeyang/RCLM (created 2026-07-28 by first author Yang Li, MIT LICENSE, documented embedding API) is linked from the Zhang lab RCLM page. Its rclm/Model.py matches DRfold2's RNALM2/Model.py except for the import line, and DRfold2 loads a checkpoint of the same name (model_hub/RCLM/epoch_67000). Parameter count, training corpus and batch count match the PLOS Biology methods. Limits: the checkpoint is served from a README-linked Google Drive file, not from GitHub files or releases, and no weight licence is stated. The lab page says the checkpoint is in the repository or its Releases page; neither holds it. The method is described only inside the DRfold2 paper (no RCLM-specific paper found in Europe PMC or arXiv). Developers suggest reuse beyond structure tasks; this is not benchmarked, and no third-party reuse of RCLM embeddings was found. The repository is young (five commits on one day, no releases). Inference was not run and no checkpoint was downloaded. DRfold (2023) contains no RCLM and is split off as a residual hold. 'DRfold' is kept as a historical alias for ID continuity only. | Evoformer-style RNA language model (about 47.5M parameters) pretrained on about 30 million RNAcentral sequences with a masked composite-likelihood objective. Preprint versions: v1 2025-05-13, v2 2025-06-06, v3 2026-10-05 (CC BY 4.0, no journal version recorded in the bioRxiv API). Year 2025 reflects first public release; the v3 preprint and the Trias-Small checkpoint (created 2026-10-01) are the current artifacts. Limits: (1) the paper's Code availability statement names only github.com/lareaulab/Trias and huggingface.co/lareaulab/Trias - Trias-Small is treated as author-endorsed because it sits under the same lareaulab account, cross-links the main card, and matches the paper's 'Trias-small' architecture description, but it is not named in the paper; (2) checkpoints were listed via the hub API, never downloaded or loaded, so weight integrity is unverified; (3) no tagged release exists, so there is no version-pinned artifact; (4) the training corpus is not redistributed (data.zip in the repo holds figure and benchmark data only); (5) the reported expression advantages are the authors' own wet-lab results - H.B. and M.F. hold equity in TriasBio, Inc., L.F.L. is an advisor, and the company funded the experimental validation; the disclosure carries no licence restriction. | AlphaFold-Multimer | 2021 | Verified | Extension of AlphaFold2 trained specifically on multimeric inputs to predict multi-chain protein complex (protein-protein assembly) structures; the standard pre-AlphaFold3 complex-prediction method. | Multi-chain protein FASTA + paired/unpaired MSAs -> protein complex 3D structure with interface confidence (ipTM/pTM) | Open code + weights | [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2021.10.04.463034) · [GitHub](https://github.com/google-deepmind/alphafold) | Protein-protein complex prediction, interface/epitope modeling, PPI screening, assembly hypotheses |
| AlphaFold2 | 2021 | Verified | MSA- and template-based deep learning model that predicts highly accurate single-chain protein 3D structures; the breakthrough CASP14 system and foundation for most downstream structure workflows and the AlphaFold DB. | Protein sequence + MSA/templates -> 3D protein structure + per-residue confidence (pLDDT); pTM-head models also output predicted TM-score (pTM) and pairwise predicted aligned error (PAE) | Open code + weights | [Nature](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-021-03819-2) · [GitHub](https://github.com/google-deepmind/alphafold) | Monomer structure prediction, structural annotation, input to docking/design pipelines, source of structural embeddings, confidence-aware filtering and ranking of predictions (pLDDT tracks lDDT-Cα; pTM tracks TM-score) |
| ATOMICA | 2025 | Verified | SE(3)-equivariant geometric foundation model pretrained on 2 million molecular interaction interfaces from PDB and CSD to learn universal representations of intermolecular interactions across all biomolecular modality pairs. | 3D molecular interaction interface (protein, nucleic acid, ligand, ion) -> interaction embeddings and interface representations | Open code + weights | [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2025.04.02.646906v1) · [GitHub](https://github.com/mims-harvard/ATOMICA) | protein-ligand binding prediction, drug discovery, molecular docking, biomolecular interaction modeling, interface representation learning |
| Boltz-1 / Boltz-2 ⭐ | 2025 | Verified | Open biomolecular interaction model family; Boltz-2 adds affinity modeling. | Biomolecular complex specification -> structure +/- affinity | Open code + weights | [Boltz-1 (PMC)](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11601547/) · [Boltz-2 (bioRxiv)](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2025.06.14.659707v1) · [GitHub](https://github.com/jwohlwend/boltz) | Complex prediction, virtual screening, affinity-aware molecular design |
| Chai-1 ⭐ | 2024 | Verified | Multimodal structure model for proteins, small molecules, DNA, RNA, glycosylations, and more. | FASTA/SMILES/CCD-like inputs -> complex 3D structure | Open code + weights | [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2024.10.10.615955v1) · [GitHub](https://github.com/chaidiscovery/chai-lab) · [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/chaidiscovery/chai-1) | Open AlphaFold3-like complex prediction, ligand/protein/nucleic-acid modeling |
| DiffDock | — | Verified | Diffusion model for protein-ligand docking. | Protein structure + ligand -> ligand pose(s) | Open code + weights | [ICLR](https://openreview.net/forum?id=kKF8_K-mBbS) · [GitHub](https://github.com/gcorso/DiffDock) | Docking, pose generation, blind docking workflows |
| DiffDock-L | 2024 | Verified | Generalizing version of the DiffDock diffusion docking model using confidence-bootstrapping self-training for much better cross-pocket generalization (DockGen success 10%->24%). | Protein structure + ligand -> ranked ligand binding poses | Open code + weights | [OpenReview](https://openreview.net/forum?id=UfBIxpTK10) · [GitHub](https://github.com/gcorso/DiffDock) | Blind molecular docking, pose generation, docking generalization to novel protein domains (DockGen benchmark) |
| ESMFold2 | 2026 | Verified | ESMC-6B-conditioned diffusion model for all-atom prediction of proteins, nucleic acids, ligands, complexes, and binder candidates. | Biomolecular sequence/specification with optional MSA -> all-atom structure | Open code + weights (MIT); local and Biohub Platform inference | [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.03.729735v1) · [GitHub](https://github.com/Biohub/esm) · [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/biohub/ESMFold2) | Fast structure prediction, complex modeling, interaction and binder assessment |
| FlowDock | 2025 | Verified | Geometric flow-matching generative model for simultaneous protein-ligand docking (apo-to-holo) and binding-affinity prediction. | Protein sequence(s) + ligand SMILES -> protein-ligand complex structure + predicted binding affinity | Open code + weights | [Oxford Acad.](https://academic.oup.com/bioinformatics/article/41/Supplement_1/i198/8199366) · [GitHub](https://github.com/BioinfoMachineLearning/FlowDock) | Apo-state blind docking, generative co-folding, affinity estimation for virtual screening |
| IntelliFold-2 | 2026 | Verified | Architectural refinement of IntelliFold surpassing AlphaFold3 on FoldBench via latent-space scaling in Pairformer, stochastic atomization, and policy-guided diffusion sampling for biomolecular structure prediction. | protein/nucleic acid/ligand sequences and features -> all-atom 3D complex structures | Open code + weights | [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.02.09.704787v1) · [GitHub](https://github.com/IntelliGen-AI/IntelliFold) | protein structure prediction, biomolecular complex modeling, drug target analysis, protein-ligand interaction studies |
| OpenFold ⭐ | 2024 | Verified | Open reimplementation/training framework for AlphaFold2-style protein structure prediction. | Protein sequence/MSA/templates -> 3D protein structure | Open code + weights | [Nature Methods](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41592-024-02272-z) · [GitHub](https://github.com/aqlaboratory/openfold) | Open AF2 reproduction, training, structure prediction |

| Protenix ⭐ | 2025 | Verified | ByteDance's trainable, fully open-source PyTorch reproduction of AlphaFold3 for all-atom biomolecular complex structure prediction; the v1 release (2026) added protein-template integration and RNA MSA support to match or exceed AF3 on several benchmarks, and v2 (2026) adds large antibody-antigen gains, GPCR hit discovery, and zero-shot antibody design. | JSON spec of protein/RNA/DNA/ligand (+optional MSA, templates, atom-level constraints) -> all-atom complex 3D structure | Open code + weights | [bioRxiv](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2025.01.08.631967v1) · [bioRxiv (v1)](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.02.05.703733v1) · [bioRxiv (v2)](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.04.10.717613v1) · [GitHub](https://github.com/bytedance/Protenix) | Open AF3-style complex prediction, antibody-antigen modeling, drug-target structures, retraining/fine-tuning research |

Four ODesign base checkpoints (~4.3 GB each) plus four OInvFold inverse-folding checkpoints and a separate ODesign-AB checkpoint are public, ungated and tagged apache-2.0; no weights downloaded. The GitHub repo was renamed from The-Institute-for-AI-Molecular-Design/ODesign to OTeam-AI4S/ODesign (old path redirects); the Hugging Face org keeps the old name and is official because the repo's own downloader points at it. GitHub licence detection reports NOASSERTION because the Apache 2.0 text carries a custom header; the file grants commercial use. Not inspected: the Google Drive inference/training data folder (no licence seen, ~850 GB archive) and the web server's own terms (JS-only page), so neither is evidence of open access. Default training initialises from an external Protenix v0.5.0 checkpoint. Unrefereed preprint: Europe PMC search returned no journal version on 2026-10-10. Limits: OpenBind-0 has a consortium announcement but no technical report, so the preview-2 report remains the only technical document; the release notes spell the checkpoint 'openbind-2025-06-03-174k' while code and docs use 2025-06-30; the README still brands the release OpenFold3-preview; the technical-report URL now redirects to portal.openfold.io. | CDDD (Continuous and Data-Driven molecular Descriptors) | 2019 | Verified | Translation (seq2seq autoencoder) model mapping between equivalent SMILES to learn a fixed-size continuous latent representation usable as molecular descriptors and for generation. | SMILES -> continuous latent descriptor vector (and back to SMILES via decoder) | Open code + weights | [RSC](https://pubs.rsc.org/en/content/articlehtml/2019/sc/c8sc04175j) · [GitHub](https://github.com/jrwnter/cddd) | Descriptor extraction for QSAR/property prediction, latent-space molecular optimization/generation |

Limits: the OpenReview PDF returned 403, so parameter counts come from the repository README and the paper text was not inspected; the README's 'model weights' links in two places point to the SMI-TED hub page. | Mamba-based (state-space) encoder-decoder chemical foundation model pretrained on 91M PubChem SMILES (~4B tokens); the authors describe 336M and 8x336M variants, and one pretrained checkpoint is public on Hugging Face. | SMILES -> embeddings or reconstructed/generated SMILES | Open code + weights (Apache-2.0), ungated on Hugging Face; GitHub repository archived (read-only) since 2026-10-07 | [Paper](https://research.ibm.com/publications/a-mamba-based-foundation-model-for-chemistry) · [GitHub](https://github.com/IBM/materials) · [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/ibm-research/materials.smi_ssed) | Quantum-property prediction, molecular reconstruction, classification/regression, synthesis-yield prediction |
| SMILES-BERT | 2019 | Verified with limits: Paper; official code. Action: upgrade from paper-only to public code, explicitly noting that pretrained weights remain unreleased. | Early transformer encoder pretrained on large unlabeled SMILES via a masked SMILES-recovery objective, then fine-tuned for molecular property prediction. | SMILES tokens -> embeddings / property predictions | Public source code; pretrained weights not released | [Paper](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3307339.3342186) · [Code](https://github.com/uta-smile/SMILES-BERT) | Molecular property prediction, transfer learning from unlabeled chemical data |
| Zatom-1 | 2026 | Verified with limits: Workshop paper (ICLR 2026 FM4Science) on arXiv. Licence labels differ across distributions but all are permissive: LBNL modified-BSD for code (the paper says 'permissive BSD'), an MIT tag in the HF card YAML (no LICENSE file in the HF repo), and CC BY 4.0 on the Zenodo record. The README downloads checkpoints from Zenodo; HF is an author-org mirror (org members are paper authors). Chemistry/materials model whose link to biology runs through small and drug-like molecules and an OMol25 checkpoint. Successor Zatom-2 (October 2026) is held for missing artifacts. Weights not executed. | Transformer (80M-300M parameters) trained with a multimodal flow-matching objective over discrete atom types and continuous 3D geometries, jointly pretrained on QM9 small molecules and MP20 crystals, with further GEOM-Drugs, QMOF and OMol25 pretraining checkpoints; the generative pretraining initialises downstream property, energy and force prediction. | Sampling from noise -> 3D molecules or crystals (atom types, coordinates, lattice); 3D molecular or crystal structure -> property, energy and force predictions via finetuned heads | LBNL modified-BSD code licence; public ungated weights on Hugging Face (card: MIT) and Zenodo (record: CC BY 4.0); no hosted API | [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22251) · [GitHub](https://github.com/Zatom-AI/zatom) · [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/Zatom-AI/Zatom-1) · [Zenodo weights](https://zenodo.org/records/19766997) | 3D small-molecule and crystal generation, molecular and materials property prediction, energy/force prediction, transfer learning |

Small model (d=128, 6 layers, 8 heads). The pretrained checkpoint (save/pretrain_run_dir/checkpoints__uniform/cytobert_epoch_50.pt, 4.9 MB) was seen in the Zenodo zip preview of the 9.2 GB archive, not downloaded. The paper evaluates only sample-level classification on 7 CyTOF datasets; CytoBERT had the best average rank under grouped splits, but differences between models were not significant (Friedman p=0.093). The repository's cell-classification tutorial uses a separate cell_tasks checkpoint (epoch 100) also shipped in the zip, which the paper does not describe. The MIT LICENSE names 'Anonymous' as copyright holder. Limits: weights were not downloaded; the technical report is author-hosted, not peer-reviewed and has no DOI; benchmarks are author-run. Atlas evidence was not used. Custom inference code ships inside the gated repositories; the technical report announces weights only, and no public training-code repository or pretraining-data release was found. | [Technical report (Aignostics, PDF)](https://cdn.prod.website-files.com/67adb01f31489469b513304a/6a74e2f9f1d4aef06489a3f1_RudolfV_2_260806.pdf) · [Hugging Face: RudolfV 2 (gated)](https://huggingface.co/Aignostics/RudolfV-2) · [Hugging Face: RudolfV 2-B (gated)](https://huggingface.co/Aignostics/RudolfV-2-B) · [Hugging Face: RudolfV 2-S (gated)](https://huggingface.co/Aignostics/RudolfV-2-S) | Tissue and tumor classification, grading, biomarker prediction, slide-level modeling via multiple-instance learning |

The README places pretrained weights, fine-tuned weights and formatted data in author Zenodo deposit 20755048 (open, labelled MIT, one 9.3 GB tarball); only the first archive entries were listed, so the checkpoint itself was not seen. Gene embeddings are initialised from scFoundation, and the official code loads the scFoundation checkpoint (downloaded separately; BioMap licence, non-commercial research only), hence noncommercial=true despite the MIT labels. Pretraining corpus available on request only. Several unrelated 'STFormer' models exist. | Transformer foundation model for spatial transcriptomics that adds biased cross-attention to ligand genes in each cell's spatial niche. Gene embeddings are initialised from scFoundation, and the model was pretrained on about 4.1 million samples obtained by cell-type deconvolution of public human Visium datasets. | Single-cell or spot expression plus ligand-gene expression from the spatial niche -> niche-aware gene embeddings and pooled cell embeddings | Open code (MIT); pretrained and fine-tuned weights in a 9.3 GB Zenodo archive labelled MIT; building the model requires scFoundation weights (BioMap non-commercial research licence); pretraining corpus on request | [Cell Reports Methods](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.crmeth.2026.101612) · [bioRxiv (2024)](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2024.09.27.615337v1) · [GitHub](https://github.com/csh3/stFormer) · [Zenodo (weights + data)](https://zenodo.org/records/20755048) | Cell clustering and batch-effect correction, cell type prediction, gene function prediction, in silico ligand-receptor perturbation; case studies on spatial domain and signalling-ligand identification |

| ELF · NC | 2026 | Verified with limits: The code and weights are labelled GPLv3 but also restricted to non-commercial academic use, which is internally inconsistent, and neither repository has a licence file; the record treats ELF as non-commercial. The paper states 14.3M parameters, but the Hugging Face inference encoder holds 151,304 (config.json). Its layers match the paper's Methods and the card calls it the trained momentum encoder, but I did not check that it is equivalent to the paper-linked Google Drive checkpoint, because that would mean downloading weights. ELF needs features from five gated third-party tile encoders. The Cancer Cell version was checked through Crossref, Europe PMC and the PMC full text (PMC13564370). | Slide-level pathology encoder from the Li lab (Stanford): a multi-head gated attention-MIL aggregator pretrained with MoCo v3 contrastive learning and auxiliary weak supervision on 53,699 WSIs from 20 anatomical sites. It turns tile embeddings from five third-party pathology foundation models (UNI, CONCH v1.5, Prov-GigaPath, Virchow2, H-optimus-0) into slide-level representations, which are concatenated into one ensemble embedding. | CLAM tile embeddings at 10x from UNI, CONCH v1.5, Prov-GigaPath, Virchow2 or H-optimus-0 -> per-model slide embedding (native dimension and 768-d) plus attention weights; the paper ensemble concatenates the five per-model slide embeddings | Public code (GitHub) and ungated slide-encoder weights (an inference-only file on Hugging Face; the paper links a Google Drive training checkpoint). Both are described as GPLv3 with an added non-commercial academic-use restriction, and neither repository has a licence file. Inputs require five gated tile encoders, three of which (UNI, CONCH v1.5, Virchow2) are under CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | [Cancer Cell](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ccell.2026.08.008) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16085) · [GitHub](https://github.com/lilab-stanford/ELF) · [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/luoxd96/ELF) | Linear-probe cancer classification and subtyping, molecular biomarker detection, and prediction of response to chemotherapy, targeted therapy and immunotherapy in small cohorts |

MIT licence confirmed by the GitHub licence API and the CSBJ abstract (DOI 10.34133/csbj.0129). noncommercial=false covers the framework only. A GitHub licence spot-check of 46 linked tool repos found no non-commercial repo licence but did find copyleft (PLIP, Open Babel GPL-2.0; VespaG GPL-3.0; xtb LGPL-3.0). Weight terms of most integrated models were not audited. The publisher DOI landing returns 403 to scripted access, so PMC is used. Unrelated GitHub repos share the name BioPipelines; the official one is locbp-uzh/biopipelines. No install or execution test. | University of Zurich Python framework that chains structure-generation, sequence-design, structure-prediction, docking and analysis tools (over 40 in the CSBJ paper, 70+ in current docs; e.g. RFdiffusion, ProteinMPNN, AlphaFold, Boltz-2) into multistep workflows; the same script runs interactively in Jupyter/Colab or as generated batch jobs on HPC clusters. It orchestrates separately installed tools and does not host models. | Python workflow script + protein/ligand inputs (PDB structures, sequences, SMILES) -> generated bash jobs run in Jupyter/Colab or on SLURM/LSF/PBS clusters, producing designed structures and sequences plus result tables | Open MIT framework code; no hosted service and no bundled weights. | Mol-Instructions | 2024 | Verified | Large-scale biomolecular instruction dataset (~2M instructions: molecule, protein, biotext tasks) with released LLaMA/LLaMA2/LLaMA3-based instruction-tuned models for biomolecular LLMs. | Instruction + molecule (SELFIES/SMILES)/protein sequence -> task output (property, description, designed molecule/protein) | Open code + weights | [OpenReview](https://openreview.net/forum?id=Tlsdsb6l9n) · [GitHub](https://github.com/zjunlp/Mol-Instructions) | Instruction-tuning biomolecular LLMs, molecule/protein design and description, benchmark for bio-LLM instruction following |
| FoldBench | 2025 | Verified | Low-homology benchmark of biological assemblies across all-atom structure-prediction tasks involving proteins, nucleic acids, ligands, and interactions. | Predicted assemblies + references -> quality and generalization metrics | Open benchmark targets + evaluation code + samples (MIT); public results | [Nature Communications](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-025-67127-3) · [GitHub](https://github.com/BEAM-Labs/FoldBench) | All-atom structure-prediction evaluation and generalization testing |
| GENEB | 2026 | Verified | Diagnostic benchmark that linear-probes frozen representations from 40 genomic foundation models across 100 DNA classification tasks in 13 functional categories, under full-data, 10-shot, and 1-shot regimes. | Frozen genomic-model embeddings + DNA classification tasks -> per-task, per-category and overall MCC, accuracy and macro-F1 scores | Open harness + task data (Apache-2.0); public ungated leaderboard | [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04525) · [GitHub](https://github.com/darlednik/GENEB) · [Hugging Face task data](https://huggingface.co/datasets/darlednik/geneb-tasks) · [Leaderboard](https://huggingface.co/spaces/darlednik/geneb-leaderboard) | Genomic model selection, head-to-head comparison of DNA language models, few-shot representation evaluation |

Unsigned S3 listings return objects for the metadata bundle (7 files), all four image stores, 16 embedding variants, cell/nuclei masks and Target-2 artifacts; an independent spot check of 12 random sites from the release site index (2 per source) returned HTTP 200 for all 252 HEAD requests across the 4 image stores, 16 embedding variants and the MQ cell-mask store. Data licence re-verified: Cell Painting Gallery is CC0 1.0 gallery-wide (AWS Open Data registry); the README defers data terms to the gallery and JUMP. noncommercial set to true because the openphenom-* embedding variants are outputs of OpenPhenom, whose Recursion Non-Commercial EULA (section 7) requires shared Derivative Technology to carry essentially equivalent non-commercial terms; this conflicts with the gallery-wide CC0 label and is unresolved. DINOv2 (Apache-2.0), MorphEm (MIT) and SubCell code (MIT) carry no NC terms. Limits: the documented image and embedding paths (images/2026_jump_lite_v1.0/..., <model>-<codec>/2026_jump_lite_v1.0/) return no objects; the actual paths are images/jump_lite_v1.0/images_compressed/ and <model>-<codec>/jump_lite/v1.0/, so these may still change. The GitHub README and arXiv v3 still say the release awaits gallery promotion. Paper is a preprint (arXiv v3, submitted to WACV 2027). Nahual is described as open-source in the paper but has no licence file, pyproject licence field or PyPI licence. Full object counts were not verified. | Compact, analysis-ready subset of the JUMP Cell Painting dataset (655,101 image sites from 551 plates in six sources) with lossless and JPEG XL-compressed image stores, per-site embeddings from DINOv2 (plus a randomly initialised baseline), MorphEm, OpenPhenom and SubCell, Cellpose cell and nuclei masks and perturbation metadata, for benchmarking cell-image representations. Released with Nahual, a framework for deploying representation models reproducibly. | Compressed Cell Painting images, per-site embeddings and perturbation metadata -> phenotypic-activity and consistency benchmark metrics for cell-image representation methods | MIT harness code (GitHub; Zenodo-archived v1.0/v1.1). Images, Cellpose masks, metadata and per-site embeddings download without credentials from the Cell Painting Gallery, whose gallery-wide licence is CC0 1.0; the release README defers data terms to the gallery and JUMP and has no release-specific licence. Non-commercial terms apply to at least one artifact: the three OpenPhenom embedding variants are outputs of a model under Recursion's Non-Commercial EULA, which requires shared derivatives to carry equivalent terms. CC0 index tables on Zenodo. Nahual has no licence (all rights reserved by default). | [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07632) · [GitHub](https://github.com/afermg/JUMP_lite) · [Zenodo code archive v1.1](https://zenodo.org/records/22266219) · [Zenodo code archive v1.0](https://zenodo.org/records/21779243) · [Cell Painting Gallery (data)](https://registry.opendata.aws/cellpainting-gallery/) · [Release README in the gallery](https://cellpainting-gallery.s3.amazonaws.com/cpg0016-jump/source_all/workspace/publication_data/2026_jump_lite/metadata/v1.0/README.md) · [Index tables (Zenodo)](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.18705140) · [Nahual (GitHub)](https://github.com/afermg/nahual) | Benchmarking cell-image representation and foundation models, image-compression sensitivity analysis, morphological-profiling method comparison |

## Evidence and reuse boundary

486 records: 247 verified and 239 with visible limitations. October review covers new and promoted entries, targeted corrections and all previously held records; older verification dates are preserved. 37 candidates remain under review. Check each record's access and licence limitations before use.

The catalog records discovery and evidence. It does not grant rights to external artifacts. Code, weights, data and hosted services can have different terms; institutional, data-protection, ethics and domain review still apply.

Catalog data, metadata, and original documentation are licensed under CC BY 4.0. Maintenance/build code is MIT licensed. External resources, logos, and trademarks retain their own terms.

Machine-readable distributions: `models_final.json`, `schema.json`, and `metadata.jsonld`.
